* Working Of the Project

This section provides a comprehensive technical documentation of the Blind Navigation System's architecture, processing pipeline, and functionality. The system operates as a client-server application where the mobile client captures video frames and the GPU-accelerated server performs real-time object detection, depth estimation, tracking, and navigation guidance.

** System Architecture Overview

*** High-Level Architecture

The system implements a distributed client-server architecture with the following components:

**** Server-Side (Flask Backend)
- YOLO Object Detection Engine (YOLOv8x trained on Open Images V7)
- Depth Estimation Service (Depth Anything V2 Small by ByteDance)
- Object Tracking System (DeepSORT with MobileNetV2 embeddings)
- Reactive Navigation Engine (Dynamic obstacle avoidance)
- Camera Calibration Service (Motion-based depth calibration)
- RESTful API Endpoints (JSON-based communication)

**** Client-Side (Browser Application)
- Camera Capture Interface (MediaStream API with gyroscope integration)
- Real-Time Detection Visualization (Canvas-based rendering with bounding boxes)
- Audio Feedback System (Web Speech API with spatial audio)
- Voice Command Recognition (Web Speech Recognition API)
- Navigation UI (Interactive pathfinding with visual and audio guidance)
- Motion Calibration Module (Accelerometer-based depth calibration)

*** Communication Protocol

The client and server communicate via HTTPS using JSON payloads:[fn:16]

**** Request Flow
1. Client captures video frame from camera (640x480 or higher resolution)
2. Frame encoded as Base64 JPEG with optional gyroscope orientation data
3. HTTP POST request sent to /detect_image endpoint with motion calibration data
4. Server processes frame and returns detection results with tracking and depth
5. Client renders bounding boxes, plays audio announcements, and updates navigation

**** Data Exchange Format
#+BEGIN_SRC json
{
  "image": "data:image/jpeg;base64,/9j/4AAQSkZJRg...",
  "tracking_enabled": true,
  "orientation": {
    "alpha": 45.2,
    "beta": 12.8,
    "gamma": -3.4
  },
  "motion_calibration": {
    "scale": 0.4074,
    "confidence": 0.85
  }
}
#+END_SRC

**** Response Structure
#+BEGIN_SRC json
{
  "success": true,
  "detections": [
    {
      "track_id": 42,
      "class_name": "person",
      "confidence": 0.89,
      "bbox": [120, 80, 340, 450],
      "center": [230, 265],
      "distance": 2.8,
      "distance_confidence": 0.75,
      "distance_method": "depth-anything-v2",
      "movement": {
        "speed": "medium",
        "direction": "moving_closer"
      },
      "collision": {
        "collision_risk": true,
        "time_to_collision": 3.2
      }
    }
  ],
  "image_size": [640, 480],
  "processing_time_ms": 82.4
}
#+END_SRC

** Server-Side Processing Pipeline

The server implements a multi-stage processing pipeline optimized for real-time performance on GPU hardware.

*** Stage 1: Request Reception and Image Decoding

**** Endpoint: /detect_image (detection.py:40-430)
The main detection endpoint handles incoming video frames:[fn:17]

***** Request Validation
- Validates JSON payload contains base64-encoded image data
- Creates or retrieves Flask session ID for tracking continuity
- Extracts optional motion calibration data (scale and confidence)
- Logs request headers with Cookie data filtered to prevent console spam

***** Image Decoding and Preprocessing
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Decode base64 image (detection.py:78-87)
image_data = data['image'].split(',')[1]  # Remove data:image/jpeg;base64,
image_bytes = base64.b64decode(image_data)
image = Image.open(BytesIO(image_bytes))
image_np = np.array(image)
image_bgr = cv2.cvtColor(image_np, cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
#+END_SRC

***** Gyroscope-Based Rotation Correction
The system supports automatic image rotation using device gyroscope data:
- Reads alpha/beta/gamma orientation angles from request
- Calculates rotation angle using get_rotation_from_gyroscope()
- Applies 0, 90, 180, or 270 degree rotation to correct for device orientation
- Updates image dimensions after rotation for correct coordinate mapping

*** Stage 2: Object Detection with YOLO

**** Model Loading and Initialization (yolo_detector.py:19-175)

***** Singleton Pattern Implementation
The system uses a singleton detector instance to avoid loading the model multiple times:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Global detector instance
_detector = None

def get_detector(model_path='yolov8x-oiv7.pt', force_cpu=True):
    global _detector
    if _detector is None:
        _detector = YOLODetector(model_path=model_path, force_cpu=force_cpu)
    return _detector
#+END_SRC

***** Model Format Support
The detector supports three model formats with automatic format detection:
- PyTorch (.pt) - Original format, good accuracy but slower (200-400ms CPU)
- ONNX (.onnx) - Cross-platform format, moderate speed (100-200ms CPU)
- TensorRT (.engine) - GPU-optimized format, fastest (30-50ms GPU, 10x speedup)[fn:18]

***** GPU Acceleration Configuration
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Device selection based on configuration
self.device = 'cpu' if force_cpu else ('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')

# Load model and configure device
self.model = YOLO(self.model_path)
if model_ext == '.engine':
    # TensorRT engines are pre-compiled for specific device
    logger.info("Using TensorRT engine for optimized GPU inference")
elif self.device == 'cpu':
    self.model.to('cpu')
else:
    self.model.to(self.device)
#+END_SRC

**** Inference Execution (detection.py:117-140)

***** Image Resizing for Performance
Large images are resized before inference to improve speed:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Optimize: Resize image if too large to speed up inference
image_bgr, scale = resize_image_for_inference(image_bgr, MAX_IMAGE_DIMENSION)
# MAX_IMAGE_DIMENSION = 640 pixels (constants.py:64)
#+END_SRC

***** Detection Parameters
The YOLO model runs with optimized thresholds:
- Confidence Threshold: 0.30 (30% minimum confidence for detections)
- IOU Threshold: 0.65 (Non-Maximum Suppression overlap threshold)
- Max Detections: 50 (Maximum objects per frame)

***** Inference Call
#+BEGIN_SRC python
results = detector.detect(
    image_bgr,
    conf=0.30,
    iou=0.65,
    max_det=50
)
#+END_SRC

Performance metrics:
- CPU (Intel/AMD): 200-400ms per frame (1-3 FPS)
- GPU RTX 5060 with TensorRT: 30-50ms per frame (10-16 FPS)

**** Detection Processing and Filtering (detection.py:143-206)

***** Raw Detection Extraction
The system extracts detection data from YOLO results:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
for r in results:
    boxes = r.boxes
    if boxes is not None:
        for box in boxes:
            class_id = int(box.cls[0])
            class_name = detector.model.names[class_id]
            bbox_coords = box.xyxy[0].tolist()  # [x1, y1, x2, y2]
            confidence = float(box.conf[0])
#+END_SRC

***** Class Blacklisting
Certain object classes are filtered out as they are not useful for navigation:
- Blacklisted classes: house, office building, building, skyscraper, tower
- Implemented in constants.py:6-14 and filtering.py

***** Class Normalization
Similar classes are mapped to canonical names to reduce redundancy:
- man, woman, boy, girl, human face, face, head → person
- This prevents duplicate announcements for the same object
- Mapping defined in constants.py:17-28

***** Confidence Filtering
Low-confidence detections are removed to reduce false positives:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
raw_detections = filter_detections_by_confidence(raw_detections, min_confidence=0.3)
# Removes detections with confidence < 30%
#+END_SRC

***** Spatial Deduplication
Overlapping detections of the SAME class are deduplicated:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
raw_detections = deduplicate_same_class_detections(raw_detections, iou_threshold=0.5)
# IOU > 0.5 = same object detected twice
#+END_SRC

This is critical because YOLO may detect "person" multiple times for the same individual, causing audio spam.

*** Stage 3: Depth Estimation

The system uses Depth Anything V2 Small for monocular depth estimation, enabling accurate distance measurements without specialized depth sensors.

**** Depth Estimator Initialization (depth_estimator.py)

***** Lazy Loading Pattern
The depth estimator is initialized on first use to avoid startup delays:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
_depth_estimator = None

def get_depth_estimator(force_cpu=False):
    global _depth_estimator
    if _depth_estimator is None:
        _depth_estimator = DepthEstimator(force_cpu=force_cpu)
    return _depth_estimator
#+END_SRC

***** Model Configuration
- Model: Depth Anything V2 Small by ByteDance Doubao Team
- Input Resolution: 256x256 pixels (downsampled for 2x speed boost)
- Device: GPU with FP16 mixed precision for RTX GPUs (2x faster inference)
- Performance: 14ms on GPU, 50-80ms on CPU
- Architecture: Vision Transformer-based encoder-decoder

***** Async Processing Pipeline
Depth estimation runs in a background thread to prevent blocking the main detection loop:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Start async depth computation (non-blocking)
depth_estimator.start_async_depth(image_bgr, request_id)

# Main detection continues immediately without waiting
# Depth results retrieved later via request_id
#+END_SRC

This reduces perceived latency from 150-300ms (blocking) to 12-20ms (async).

**** Distance Estimation with Depth Maps (detection.py:174-189)

For each detected object, the system estimates its distance using depth data:

***** Three-Tier Depth Estimation Strategy

1. PRIMARY: Deep Learning Depth Estimation
   - Uses Depth Anything V2 to generate per-pixel depth map
   - Samples depth values within object bounding box
   - Converts relative depth to metric distance using motion calibration
   - Confidence score based on depth map variance and calibration quality

2. FALLBACK 1: Known Object Heights Database
   - Database of 500+ object classes with typical real-world heights
   - Uses pinhole camera model: distance = (known_height × focal_length) / bbox_height_pixels
   - Example: chair (1.0m tall), person (1.7m tall), door (2.0m tall)
   - Defined in constants.py:81-697

3. FALLBACK 2: Bounding Box Heuristic
   - Estimates distance from bbox size and vertical position in frame
   - Larger bbox + lower position = closer object
   - Used when depth unavailable and object height unknown
   - Least accurate but better than no estimate

***** Distance Estimation Call
#+BEGIN_SRC python
distance_info = depth_estimator.estimate_distance_with_depth(
    image_bgr,
    bbox_coords,
    normalized_class_name,
    motion_scale=motion_scale,        # From motion calibration
    motion_confidence=motion_confidence
)

# Returns:
{
    'distance': 2.8,              # meters
    'confidence': 0.75,           # 0-1 score
    'method': 'depth-anything-v2' # estimation method used
}
#+END_SRC

**** Motion-Based Depth Calibration (camera_calibration.py)

Depth maps provide relative depth (closer vs farther) but not absolute metric depth. The system uses motion calibration to convert relative depth to meters.

***** Calibration Process
1. User walks forward while holding phone steady
2. System detects step motion using accelerometer data
3. Tracks how objects move in frame during forward motion
4. Calculates scale factor: pixel_movement / real_world_movement
5. Stores calibration per camera for reuse across sessions

***** Calibration Validation
- Minimum confidence threshold: 0.25 (lowered from 0.3 for better coverage)
- Rejects calibrations with excessive noise or contradictory data
- Falls back to pinhole model if calibration confidence too low

*** Stage 4: Object Tracking with DeepSORT

The system implements DeepSORT object tracking to maintain consistent object identities across frames, enabling movement analysis and collision prediction.

**** Tracking Manager Architecture (object_tracker.py)

***** Session-Based Tracker Instances
Each user session gets its own DeepSORT tracker instance:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Trackers stored per session ID
self.trackers = {}  # session_id -> DeepSORT instance

def update_tracks(self, session_id, detections, frame):
    if session_id not in self.trackers:
        self.trackers[session_id] = DeepSort(
            max_age=10,              # Max frames without detection
            n_init=2,                # Detections needed to confirm track
            nms_max_overlap=0.7,     # Spatial overlap threshold
            max_cosine_distance=0.25 # Appearance similarity threshold
        )
#+END_SRC

***** Tracking Parameters Optimization
The tracking parameters are tuned for dynamic navigation scenarios:
- max_age=10: Tracks deleted after 10 frames without detection (prevents ghost tracks)
- n_init=2: Tracks confirmed after 2 consecutive detections (faster initialization)
- nms_max_overlap=0.7: Overlapping detections merged if IOU > 0.7
- max_cosine_distance=0.25: Strict appearance matching (reduced from 0.4 to prevent misidentification)

**** Selective Tracking Optimization (constants.py:45-47, detection.py:215-240)

To improve performance, the system only tracks objects that need collision analysis:

***** Tracking Decision Criteria
Objects are tracked if they meet ANY of these conditions:
1. Important class: person, car, bicycle, motorcycle, truck, bus, dog, cat
2. Large enough: bbox area > 3% of frame area (close to user)
3. All others: treated as static background objects (no tracking)

***** Implementation
#+BEGIN_SRC python
if ENABLE_SELECTIVE_TRACKING:
    frame_area = image_np.shape[0] * image_np.shape[1]

    for det in raw_detections:
        bbox_area = (x2 - x1) * (y2 - y1)
        bbox_area_ratio = bbox_area / frame_area

        should_track = (
            det['class_name'] in SELECTIVE_TRACKING_CLASSES or
            bbox_area_ratio >= SELECTIVE_TRACKING_MIN_SIZE
        )
#+END_SRC

This reduces tracking overhead by 40-60% in typical scenes while maintaining collision detection for important objects.

**** Visual Embedding Extraction

***** MobileNetV2 Feature Embedder
DeepSORT uses appearance features to match objects across frames:
- Model: MobileNetV2 pretrained on ImageNet
- Input: Cropped object patch from bounding box
- Output: 128-dimensional feature vector
- Use: Calculate cosine similarity between tracks and detections

***** Three-Tier GPU Initialization
The embedder has fallback logic for GPU compatibility issues:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Try 1: GPU with FP16 mixed precision (fastest)
try:
    embedder = MobileNetV2Embedder(device='cuda', fp16=True)
except:
    # Try 2: GPU with FP32 (slower but more compatible)
    try:
        embedder = MobileNetV2Embedder(device='cuda', fp16=False)
    except:
        # Try 3: CPU fallback (slowest but always works)
        embedder = MobileNetV2Embedder(device='cpu', fp16=False)
#+END_SRC

This resolves the "Cannot copy out of meta tensor" error encountered on certain GPU configurations.

**** Track Processing and Data Enrichment (detection.py:268-333)

***** Track Information Extraction
For each confirmed track, the system extracts:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
track_id = track.track_id              # Unique ID (persistent across frames)
ltrb = track.to_ltrb()                 # Bounding box [left, top, right, bottom]
det_class = track.det_class            # Object class name
det_conf = track.det_conf              # Detection confidence
center_x = (ltrb[0] + ltrb[2]) / 2     # Center point X
center_y = (ltrb[1] + ltrb[3]) / 2     # Center point Y
#+END_SRC

***** Trajectory History
The system maintains the last 30 positions for each track:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
trajectory = tracking_manager.get_track_history(session_id, track_id)
# Returns: [[x1, y1], [x2, y2], ..., [x30, y30]]
#+END_SRC

***** Movement Analysis (collision_detector.py:1-56)
The system analyzes trajectories to calculate movement direction and speed:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
movement_info = calculate_movement(trajectory)

# Returns:
{
    'speed': 'medium',                    # slow/medium/fast/stationary
    'direction': 'moving_closer',         # moving_closer/moving_away/moving_left/moving_right
    'velocity': [dx, dy],                 # pixel displacement per frame
    'is_moving': True                     # Boolean movement flag
}
#+END_SRC

Movement classification:
- STATIONARY: < 5 pixels displacement over 10 frames
- SLOW: 2-10 pixels per frame
- MEDIUM: 10-50 pixels per frame
- FAST: > 50 pixels per frame

Direction calculation uses perspective-aware logic:
- Object moving DOWN in frame → moving_closer (getting bigger)
- Object moving UP in frame → moving_away (getting smaller)
- Object moving LEFT/RIGHT → lateral movement

***** Collision Prediction (collision_detector.py:58-120)
The system predicts collision risk by extrapolating trajectories:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
collision_info = predict_collision(
    (center_x, center_y),
    movement_info,
    image_width,
    image_height
)

# Returns:
{
    'collision_risk': True,               # Boolean collision flag
    'time_to_collision': 3.2,             # Seconds until collision
    'collision_point': [320, 450],        # Predicted collision coordinates
    'in_user_zone': False                 # Within 15% of frame center
}
#+END_SRC

Collision detection criteria:
1. Object is moving (not stationary)
2. Object trajectory intersects with user zone (15% radius around frame center)
3. Time to collision < 10 frames (approximately 1-2 seconds at 10 FPS)

***** Depth Data Inheritance
Tracks inherit depth data from matched raw detections using IoU matching:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Match track to raw detection by IoU
best_match = None
best_iou = 0.3  # Minimum IoU threshold

for det in raw_detections:
    iou = calculate_iou(ltrb, det['bbox_xyxy'])
    if iou > best_iou:
        best_iou = iou
        best_match = det

# Use matched detection's depth data
if best_match:
    distance = best_match.get('distance')
    distance_confidence = best_match.get('distance_confidence')
    distance_method = best_match.get('distance_method')
#+END_SRC

This ensures depth estimates are consistent between detection and tracking pipelines.

**** Stale Track Cleanup

***** Automatic Cleanup
The tracking system automatically removes expired tracker instances:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
# Cleanup stale trackers every 100 detection requests
if self.request_count % 100 == 0:
    current_time = time.time()
    stale_sessions = []

    for session_id, last_time in self.last_access.items():
        if current_time - last_time > 300:  # 5 minutes
            stale_sessions.append(session_id)

    for session_id in stale_sessions:
        del self.trackers[session_id]
#+END_SRC

This prevents memory leaks from abandoned user sessions.

*** Stage 5: Response Construction and Serialization

**** Final Object Assembly (detection.py:319-394)

The system constructs the final detection objects with all enriched data:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
tracked_objects.append({
    'track_id': track_id,              # Persistent tracking ID
    'class_name': det_class,           # Normalized class name
    'confidence': det_conf,            # Detection confidence (0-1)
    'bbox': [x1, y1, x2, y2],          # Bounding box coordinates
    'center': [center_x, center_y],    # Object center point
    'trajectory': trajectory,          # Last 30 positions
    'movement': movement_info,         # Speed and direction
    'collision': collision_info,       # Collision prediction
    'is_confirmed': True,              # Track confirmed by DeepSORT
    'distance': distance,              # Distance in meters
    'distance_confidence': 0.75,       # Depth estimate confidence
    'distance_method': 'depth-anything-v2'  # Method used
})
#+END_SRC

**** JSON Serialization (detection.py:395-415)

***** Type Conversion for JSON Safety
Python native types and NumPy types are not JSON-serializable, so explicit conversion is required:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
response_data = {
    'detections': tracked_objects,
    'success': True,
    'image_size': [int(image_np.shape[1]), int(image_np.shape[0])],
    'filtered_count': int(filtered_count),
    'rotation_applied': int(rotation_applied),
    'orientation_source': str(orientation_source),
    'orientation_corrected': bool(rotation_applied != 0),
    'gyroscope_available': bool('orientation' in data),
    'tracking_enabled': bool(tracking_enabled),
    'tracked_count': int(len(tracked_objects)),
    'raw_detection_count': int(len(raw_detections)),
    'collision_warnings': int(len(collision_warnings)),
    'processing_time_ms': float((time.time() - start_time) * 1000)
}
#+END_SRC

This was critical to fix the "Object of type bool_ is not JSON serializable" error encountered with NumPy boolean types.

**** Performance Logging
The system logs detailed timing information for performance monitoring:
#+BEGIN_SRC text
Received detection request
Running YOLO detection on image shape: (480, 640, 3)
model() returned in 42.3ms
Detection processing (with depth): 125.7ms
Tracking pipeline: 18.4ms
Total processing time: 186.8ms
#+END_SRC

** Client-Side Processing Pipeline

The client-side application runs in the browser and provides the user interface, camera capture, visualization, and audio feedback.

*** Stage 1: Application Initialization (main.js)

**** Module System Architecture
The application uses ES6 modules for code organization:
- audio.js - Audio feedback and text-to-speech
- camera.js - Camera capture and frame upload
- detection.js - Detection visualization and processing
- gyroscope.js - Device orientation tracking
- pathfinding.js - Navigation and path visualization
- ui.js - User interface controls
- voiceComm.js - Voice command recognition
- motionCalibration.js - Accelerometer-based calibration

**** Global State Management
Key application state is stored in window globals:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
window.lastDetections = [];         // Most recent detection results
window.pathfindingEnabled = false;  // Navigation mode active
window.navigationGoal = null;       // Target coordinates [x, y]
window.audioEnabled = true;         // Audio feedback enabled
#+END_SRC

*** Stage 2: Camera Initialization and Capture (camera.js)

**** Camera Permission Request
The system requests camera access using MediaStream API:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
async function startCamera() {
    const stream = await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({
        video: {
            facingMode: 'environment',  // Rear camera on mobile
            width: { ideal: 1280 },
            height: { ideal: 720 }
        }
    });

    videoElement.srcObject = stream;
    await videoElement.play();
}
#+END_SRC

**** HTTPS Requirement
Modern browsers require HTTPS for camera access (except localhost):
- Desktop: Works with self-signed certificates after user accepts warning
- Mobile: Varies by browser (iOS Safari most restrictive)
- Production: Requires valid CA-signed certificate or native app

**** Frame Capture Loop
The system captures frames at configurable intervals:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function captureAndSendFrame() {
    // Draw video frame to canvas
    canvas.width = videoElement.videoWidth;
    canvas.height = videoElement.videoHeight;
    ctx.drawImage(videoElement, 0, 0);

    // Convert to base64 JPEG
    const imageDataURL = canvas.toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.85);

    // Send to server
    sendDetectionRequest(imageDataURL);

    // Schedule next capture
    setTimeout(captureAndSendFrame, FRAME_INTERVAL);
}
#+END_SRC

Frame interval configuration:
- CPU mode: 800ms (1.25 FPS) - prevents request backlog
- GPU mode: 150ms (6.7 FPS) - matches server processing speed
- Configurable in timingConfig.js

*** Stage 3: Gyroscope Integration (gyroscope.js)

**** Device Orientation API
The system reads device orientation to correct image rotation:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function initializeOrientation() {
    window.addEventListener('deviceorientation', (event) => {
        gyroscopeData.alpha = event.alpha;  // Compass heading (0-360)
        gyroscopeData.beta = event.beta;    // Front-to-back tilt (-180 to 180)
        gyroscopeData.gamma = event.gamma;  // Left-to-right tilt (-90 to 90)
    });
}
#+END_SRC

**** Rotation Calculation
The system calculates the required rotation angle:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function getRotationFromGyroscope(orientation) {
    const beta = orientation.beta;
    const gamma = orientation.gamma;

    if (Math.abs(beta) > 135) return 180;  // Upside down
    if (gamma > 45) return 90;              // Landscape right
    if (gamma < -45) return -90;            // Landscape left
    return 0;                               // Portrait
}
#+END_SRC

This ensures correct bounding box coordinates regardless of device orientation.

**** iOS Permission Handling
iOS 13+ requires explicit permission for motion sensors:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
async function requestOrientationPermission() {
    if (typeof DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission === 'function') {
        const permission = await DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission();
        if (permission === 'granted') {
            initializeOrientation();
        }
    } else {
        initializeOrientation();  // Non-iOS or older iOS
    }
}
#+END_SRC

*** Stage 4: Detection Request Transmission (camera.js)

**** Request ID Tracking
To prevent stale responses, each request gets a unique ID:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
const requestId = generateUUID();
currentRequestId = requestId;  // Store for validation

const payload = {
    image: imageDataURL,
    tracking_enabled: true,
    orientation: gyroscopeData,
    motion_calibration: getMotionCalibrationData(),
    request_id: requestId
};
#+END_SRC

**** Stale Response Filtering
Responses are validated at two checkpoints:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
const response = await fetch('/detect_image', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify(payload)
});

// Checkpoint 1: After response arrives
if (requestId !== currentRequestId) {
    console.warn('Stale response - request overtaken');
    return;
}

const data = await response.json();

// Checkpoint 2: After JSON parsing
if (requestId !== currentRequestId) {
    console.warn('Stale response - discarded after parsing');
    return;
}
#+END_SRC

This fixes the issue where 10-30% of responses arrived out of order in CPU mode, causing visual lag.

*** Stage 5: Detection Visualization (detection.js)

**** Canvas Rendering
Detections are rendered on a transparent canvas overlay:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function drawDetections(detections, canvas, videoElement) {
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');

    // Clear previous frame
    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    detections.forEach(detection => {
        const [x1, y1, x2, y2] = detection.bbox;

        // Draw bounding box
        ctx.strokeStyle = getColorForClass(detection.class_name);
        ctx.lineWidth = 3;
        ctx.strokeRect(x1, y1, x2 - x1, y2 - y1);

        // Draw label
        const label = `${detection.class_name} ${(detection.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}%`;
        ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.7)';
        ctx.fillRect(x1, y1 - 25, ctx.measureText(label).width + 10, 25);
        ctx.fillStyle = '#ffffff';
        ctx.font = 'bold 14px Arial';
        ctx.fillText(label, x1 + 5, y1 - 8);
    });
}
#+END_SRC

**** Color Coding System
Different object classes are color-coded for visual distinction:
- Person: Red (#ff0000) - high priority for navigation
- Vehicle: Yellow (#ffff00) - collision hazard
- Furniture: Blue (#0000ff) - static obstacle
- Other: Green (#00ff00) - general objects

**** Trajectory Visualization
For tracked objects, the system draws movement trails:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
if (detection.trajectory && detection.trajectory.length > 1) {
    ctx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(255, 255, 0, 0.6)';
    ctx.lineWidth = 2;
    ctx.beginPath();

    detection.trajectory.forEach((point, index) => {
        if (index === 0) {
            ctx.moveTo(point[0], point[1]);
        } else {
            ctx.lineTo(point[0], point[1]);
        }
    });

    ctx.stroke();
}
#+END_SRC

**** Collision Warning Indicators
Objects with collision risk get special visual treatment:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
if (detection.collision && detection.collision.collision_risk) {
    // Red pulsing border
    ctx.strokeStyle = '#ff0000';
    ctx.lineWidth = 5;
    ctx.setLineDash([10, 5]);
    ctx.strokeRect(x1 - 5, y1 - 5, (x2 - x1) + 10, (y2 - y1) + 10);
    ctx.setLineDash([]);

    // Warning icon
    ctx.fillStyle = '#ff0000';
    ctx.font = 'bold 24px Arial';
    ctx.fillText('⚠', x2 + 10, y1 + 20);
}
#+END_SRC

*** Stage 6: Audio Feedback System (audio.js)

The audio system provides real-time spoken feedback to guide blind users through their environment.

**** Text-to-Speech Engine
The system uses the Web Speech API for natural voice output:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function speak(text, priority = 'normal') {
    const utterance = new SpeechSynthesisUtterance(text);
    utterance.rate = 1.0;      // Speech rate (0.5 to 2.0)
    utterance.pitch = 1.0;     // Pitch (0.0 to 2.0)
    utterance.volume = 1.0;    // Volume (0.0 to 1.0)

    // Select voice (prefer female voices for clarity)
    const voices = window.speechSynthesis.getVoices();
    const preferredVoice = voices.find(v =>
        v.lang === 'en-US' && v.name.includes('Female')
    );
    if (preferredVoice) utterance.voice = preferredVoice;

    window.speechSynthesis.speak(utterance);
}
#+END_SRC

**** Priority-Based Audio Queue
The system implements a three-level priority queue to prevent audio spam:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
const audioPriorities = {
    'critical': 3,  // Collision warnings, emergency stops
    'high': 2,      // Navigation instructions, object found
    'normal': 1     // General detections
};

function addToAudioQueue(announcement, priority) {
    // Critical announcements interrupt current speech
    if (priority === 'critical') {
        window.speechSynthesis.cancel();
        speak(announcement);
        return;
    }

    // High priority goes to front of queue
    if (priority === 'high') {
        audioQueue.unshift(announcement);
    } else {
        audioQueue.push(announcement);
    }

    processAudioQueue();
}
#+END_SRC

**** Rate Limiting System
To prevent overwhelming users with audio, the system implements global and per-class rate limiting:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
// Global rate limiting (audio.js:50-60)
const GLOBAL_ANNOUNCE_INTERVAL = 800;  // Minimum 800ms between any announcements
let lastGlobalAnnouncement = 0;

function canAnnounce() {
    const now = Date.now();
    if (now - lastGlobalAnnouncement < GLOBAL_ANNOUNCE_INTERVAL) {
        return false;
    }
    lastGlobalAnnouncement = now;
    return true;
}

// Per-class cooldowns
const CLASS_COOLDOWNS = {
    'person': 3000,      // Announce person at most every 3 seconds
    'car': 2000,         // Vehicles every 2 seconds
    'furniture': 5000,   // Furniture every 5 seconds
    'default': 3000      // Others every 3 seconds
};
#+END_SRC

This fixes the "audio spam" issue where 150ms detection loops with 12ms inference caused overwhelming "danger" announcements.

**** Spatial Audio Feedback
The system includes directional information in announcements:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function announceDetection(detection) {
    const direction = getObjectDirection(detection.bbox);
    const distance = detection.distance
        ? `${detection.distance.toFixed(1)} meters`
        : estimateDistance(detection.bbox);

    const announcement = `${detection.class_name} ${distance} to your ${direction}`;
    addToAudioQueue(announcement, 'normal');
}

function getObjectDirection(bbox) {
    const centerX = (bbox[0] + bbox[2]) / 2;
    const imageWidth = 640;

    const normalizedX = centerX / imageWidth;

    if (normalizedX < 0.33) return 'left';
    if (normalizedX > 0.67) return 'right';
    return 'front';
}
#+END_SRC

**** Movement Announcements
The system announces object movements with perspective-aware terminology:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
if (detection.movement && detection.movement.is_moving) {
    const movementText = {
        'moving_closer': 'approaching',
        'moving_away': 'moving away',
        'moving_left': 'moving left',
        'moving_right': 'moving right'
    }[detection.movement.direction];

    if (movementText) {
        const announcement = `${detection.class_name} ${movementText}`;
        addToAudioQueue(announcement, 'high');
    }
}
#+END_SRC

This replaces confusing screen-space terms like "moving up" with user-centric terms like "moving away".

**** Collision Warnings
High-priority warnings for collision risks:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
if (detection.collision && detection.collision.collision_risk) {
    const timeToCollision = detection.collision.time_to_collision;
    const warningText = timeToCollision < 2
        ? `Danger! ${detection.class_name} approaching fast!`
        : `Caution! ${detection.class_name} approaching in ${timeToCollision.toFixed(0)} seconds`;

    addToAudioQueue(warningText, 'critical');
}
#+END_SRC

*** Stage 7: Voice Command Recognition (voiceComm.js)

The system implements hands-free voice control using the Web Speech Recognition API.

**** Speech Recognition Initialization
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
const recognition = new webkitSpeechRecognition();
recognition.continuous = true;           // Keep listening
recognition.interimResults = false;      // Only final results
recognition.lang = 'en-US';              // Language
recognition.maxAlternatives = 1;         // Single best result

recognition.onresult = (event) => {
    const transcript = event.results[event.results.length - 1][0].transcript;
    handleVoiceCommand(transcript.toLowerCase().trim());
};
#+END_SRC

**** Supported Voice Commands
The system recognizes the following commands:

***** Navigation Control
- "start" / "begin" / "go" - Start detection and camera
- "stop" / "pause" / "halt" - Stop detection
- "find [object]" - Navigate to specific object
  - Example: "find bench", "find door", "find chair"

***** Audio Control
- "audio on" / "sound on" - Enable audio feedback
- "audio off" / "sound off" / "quiet" - Disable audio
- "what do you see" / "describe" - List visible objects

***** Pathfinding Control
- "navigate" / "navigation on" - Enable navigation mode
- "cancel navigation" / "stop navigating" - Disable navigation
- "where am I" / "location" - Announce current status

**** Find Command Implementation (main.js:160-285)
The "find" command is the most complex voice feature:

***** Command Parsing
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
// Extract object name from command
const findMatch = command.match(/find\s+(the\s+|a\s+)?(\w+)/i);
const targetObject = findMatch[2].toLowerCase();
#+END_SRC

***** Object Search with Fuzzy Matching
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
let foundObject = null;
let maxArea = 0;  // Track largest bbox (closest object)

window.lastDetections.forEach(detection => {
    const detectionName = detection.class_name.toLowerCase();

    // Exact match or partial match
    if (detectionName === targetObject ||
        detectionName.includes(targetObject) ||
        targetObject.includes(detectionName)) {

        const bboxArea = (bbox[2] - bbox[0]) * (bbox[3] - bbox[1]);

        // Find CLOSEST match (largest bbox)
        if (bboxArea > maxArea) {
            maxArea = bboxArea;
            foundObject = detection;
        }
    }
});
#+END_SRC

This fixes the bug where "find" selected the FARTHEST object instead of CLOSEST.

***** Automatic Navigation Activation
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
if (foundObject) {
    // Enable pathfinding if not already enabled
    if (!window.pathfindingEnabled) {
        togglePathfinding();
    }

    // Calculate object center in video coordinates
    const centerX = (foundObject.bbox[0] + foundObject.bbox[2]) / 2;
    const centerY = (foundObject.bbox[1] + foundObject.bbox[3]) / 2;

    // Transform to client coordinates for goal setting
    const rect = detectionCanvas.getBoundingClientRect();
    const scaleX = rect.width / videoElement.videoWidth;
    const scaleY = rect.height / videoElement.videoHeight;

    const clientX = (centerX * scaleX) + rect.left;
    const clientY = (centerY * scaleY) + rect.top;

    // Set navigation goal
    setNavigationGoal({ clientX, clientY }, detectionCanvas, videoElement);
}
#+END_SRC

This required fixing a three-stage coordinate transformation bug (video → canvas → client coordinates).

*** Stage 8: Navigation and Pathfinding (pathfinding.js)

The navigation system provides real-time obstacle avoidance guidance to help users navigate to target objects or locations.

**** Goal Setting Interface
Users can set navigation goals by tapping on the video feed:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
detectionCanvas.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!pathfindingEnabled) {
        togglePathfinding();  // Auto-enable navigation
    }

    setNavigationGoal(event, detectionCanvas, videoElement);
});

function setNavigationGoal(event, canvas, videoElement) {
    // Convert client coordinates to video coordinates
    const rect = canvas.getBoundingClientRect();
    const clickX = event.clientX - rect.left;
    const clickY = event.clientY - rect.top;

    const scaleX = videoElement.videoWidth / rect.width;
    const scaleY = videoElement.videoHeight / rect.height;

    const goalX = clickX * scaleX;
    const goalY = clickY * scaleY;

    window.navigationGoal = [goalX, goalY];

    // Visual feedback
    drawNavigationGoal(goalX, goalY, canvas);
}
#+END_SRC

**** Reactive Navigation Request
Every 500ms, the system requests navigation guidance:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
async function updateReactiveNavigation() {
    if (!window.navigationGoal || !window.lastDetections) {
        return;
    }

    const response = await fetch('/navigate_reactive', {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
        body: JSON.stringify({
            detections: window.lastDetections,
            goal: window.navigationGoal,
            image_width: 640,
            image_height: 480
        })
    });

    const guidance = await response.json();
    displayNavigationGuidance(guidance);
}

// Update every 500ms
setInterval(updateReactiveNavigation, 500);
#+END_SRC

**** Server-Side Reactive Navigation (pathfinder.py:229-510)

***** Obstacle Analysis
The system analyzes all detected objects as potential obstacles:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
for det in detections:
    bbox = det['bbox']
    obs_center = np.array([(x1 + x2) / 2, (y1 + y2) / 2])

    # Vector from user to obstacle
    to_obstacle = obs_center - np.array(start)
    distance_to_obs = np.linalg.norm(to_obstacle)
    direction_to_obs = to_obstacle / distance_to_obs

    # Use real distance from depth estimation
    real_distance = det.get('distance')  # meters

    # Calculate if obstacle is in our path
    alignment_with_goal = np.dot(direction_to_obs, goal_direction)

    obstacles.append({
        'label': det.get('class_name'),
        'distance': real_distance,          # NOW IN METERS!
        'alignment_with_goal': alignment_with_goal,
        'in_path': alignment_with_goal > 0.5  # In forward direction
    })
#+END_SRC

***** Safe Direction Calculation
The system samples 16 candidate directions to find the safest path:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
def find_safe_direction(goal_direction, obstacles, start, image_width, image_height):
    num_samples = 16
    angles = np.linspace(-np.pi/2, np.pi/2, num_samples)  # -90 to +90 degrees

    goal_angle = np.arctan2(goal_direction[1], goal_direction[0])

    best_direction = goal_direction.copy()
    best_score = -float('inf')

    for angle_offset in angles:
        test_angle = goal_angle + angle_offset
        test_direction = np.array([np.cos(test_angle), np.sin(test_angle)])

        score = 0

        # Prefer directions close to goal
        goal_alignment = np.dot(test_direction, goal_direction)
        score += goal_alignment * 10

        # Penalize based on obstacle proximity
        for obs in obstacles:
            if obs['in_path']:
                obs_direction = np.array(obs['direction'])
                alignment = np.dot(test_direction, obs_direction)

                if alignment > 0.3:  # Obstacle in this direction
                    penalty = alignment * (50 / max(obs['distance'], 1.0))
                    if obs['is_moving']:
                        penalty *= 1.5
                    score -= penalty

        if score > best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_direction = test_direction

    return best_direction
#+END_SRC

***** Voice Guidance Generation
The system generates human-readable navigation instructions:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
def generate_voice_guidance(safe_direction, goal_direction, distance_to_goal, obstacles):
    # Calculate deviation from goal
    deviation_angle = np.degrees(np.arccos(np.dot(safe_direction, goal_direction)))

    # Determine direction text
    if deviation_angle < 10:
        direction_text = "Walk straight ahead"
    elif deviation_angle < 30:
        cross = np.cross(goal_direction, safe_direction)
        side = "slightly left" if cross > 0 else "slightly right"
        direction_text = f"Bear {side}"
    else:
        cross = np.cross(goal_direction, safe_direction)
        side = "left" if cross > 0 else "right"
        direction_text = f"Turn {side}"

    # Distance text (NOW IN METERS!)
    if distance_to_goal < 2:
        distance_text = "almost there"
    elif distance_to_goal < 5:
        distance_text = f"{int(distance_to_goal)} meters"
    else:
        distance_text = f"{int(distance_to_goal)} meters ahead"

    # Check for immediate obstacles
    warnings = []
    for obs in obstacles[:3]:
        if obs['in_path'] and obs['distance'] < 3:  # Within 3 meters
            obs_dir = np.array(obs['direction'])
            cross = np.cross(safe_direction, obs_dir)
            side = "left" if cross > 0 else "right"

            label = obs['label']
            moving_text = "moving " if obs['is_moving'] else ""
            warnings.append(f"{moving_text}{label} on {side}")

    primary_message = f"{direction_text}, {distance_text}"

    return {
        'primary': primary_message,
        'warnings': warnings,
        'full_message': primary_message + (", " + ", ".join(warnings) if warnings else "")
    }
#+END_SRC

Example guidance outputs:
- "Walk straight ahead, 5 meters"
- "Bear slightly right, almost there"
- "Turn left, 8 meters ahead, moving person on right"

**** Vision-Based Position Tracking (navigation.py:123-247)

The navigation system tracks user position using visual odometry from depth-tracked objects:

***** Position Estimation Algorithm
#+BEGIN_SRC python
def estimate_camera_movement(detections, previous_detections, previous_position, image_width, image_height):
    # Match tracked objects between frames
    matched_objects = []
    for curr in detections:
        track_id = curr.get('track_id')

        for prev in previous_detections:
            if prev.get('track_id') == track_id:
                # Use objects with good distance estimates
                if (curr.get('distance') and prev.get('distance') and
                    curr.get('distance_confidence') > 0.3 and
                    prev.get('distance_confidence') > 0.3):

                    matched_objects.append({
                        'curr_center': curr_center,
                        'prev_center': prev_center,
                        'curr_distance': curr['distance'],
                        'prev_distance': prev['distance']
                    })

    # Estimate camera movement from distance changes
    movement_estimates = []
    for obj in matched_objects:
        distance_change = obj['prev_distance'] - obj['curr_distance']

        if abs(distance_change) < 0.1:  # Less than 10cm - likely noise
            continue

        # Convert to pixel movement (rough: 100 pixels ~ 1 meter)
        pixel_movement = distance_change * 100

        # Direction: from previous position toward object
        direction_to_obj = obj['prev_center'] - previous_position
        direction_to_obj = direction_to_obj / np.linalg.norm(direction_to_obj)

        # Estimate movement vector
        movement_vector = direction_to_obj * pixel_movement
        movement_estimates.append(movement_vector)

    # Weighted average of movement estimates
    weighted_movement = np.mean(movement_estimates, axis=0)
    new_position = previous_position + weighted_movement

    return new_position, confidence
#+END_SRC

This allows the system to update user position as they walk, enabling accurate navigation guidance without GPS or external tracking.

**** Path Visualization
The client visualizes the navigation path and safe direction:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
function drawNavigationPath(guidance, canvas) {
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');

    // Draw goal marker
    const [goalX, goalY] = window.navigationGoal;
    ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0, 255, 0, 0.5)';
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(goalX, goalY, 20, 0, 2 * Math.PI);
    ctx.fill();

    // Draw safe direction arrow
    if (guidance.safe_direction) {
        const userX = canvas.width / 2;
        const userY = canvas.height - 20;

        const [dirX, dirY] = guidance.safe_direction;
        const arrowLength = 100;

        const endX = userX + (dirX * arrowLength);
        const endY = userY + (dirY * arrowLength);

        ctx.strokeStyle = '#00ff00';
        ctx.lineWidth = 4;
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.moveTo(userX, userY);
        ctx.lineTo(endX, endY);
        ctx.stroke();

        // Arrow head
        const angle = Math.atan2(dirY, dirX);
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.moveTo(endX, endY);
        ctx.lineTo(endX - 15 * Math.cos(angle - Math.PI/6),
                  endY - 15 * Math.sin(angle - Math.PI/6));
        ctx.lineTo(endX - 15 * Math.cos(angle + Math.PI/6),
                  endY - 15 * Math.sin(angle + Math.PI/6));
        ctx.closePath();
        ctx.fill();
    }

    // Display guidance text
    if (guidance.guidance && guidance.guidance.full_message) {
        speak(guidance.guidance.full_message, 'high');

        // Visual text overlay
        ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.7)';
        ctx.fillRect(10, 10, canvas.width - 20, 60);
        ctx.fillStyle = '#ffffff';
        ctx.font = 'bold 20px Arial';
        ctx.fillText(guidance.guidance.primary, 20, 35);

        if (guidance.guidance.warnings.length > 0) {
            ctx.fillStyle = '#ff0000';
            ctx.font = '16px Arial';
            ctx.fillText(guidance.guidance.warnings.join(', '), 20, 55);
        }
    }
}
#+END_SRC

** Performance Optimization Techniques

*** GPU Acceleration with TensorRT

**** TensorRT Engine Conversion
The system uses TensorRT for 10x faster GPU inference:
#+BEGIN_SRC bash
# Convert YOLO ONNX to TensorRT engine
yolo export model=yolov8x-oiv7.pt format=engine device=0

# Result: yolov8x-oiv7.engine (GPU-optimized)
# Inference time: 30-50ms (vs 400-600ms on CPU)
#+END_SRC

**** Version Compatibility Requirements
TensorRT engines are highly version-sensitive:
- CUDA Version: 11.8 or 12.x
- TensorRT Version: 10.0.1 (downgraded from 10.13.3.9 for RTX 5060 compatibility)
- PyTorch Version: 2.7.1+cu128
- Engine built on SAME system where it will run

Attempting to use an engine built on a different CUDA version results in:
#+BEGIN_SRC text
RuntimeError: CUDA initialization failure with error: 35
#+END_SRC

*** Async Depth Processing

**** Background Thread Architecture
Depth estimation runs in a separate thread to avoid blocking detection:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
class AsyncDepthWorker:
    def __init__(self):
        self.queue = Queue()
        self.results = {}
        self.worker_thread = Thread(target=self._process_queue, daemon=True)
        self.worker_thread.start()

    def _process_queue(self):
        while True:
            request_id, image_bgr = self.queue.get()

            # Run depth estimation (14ms on GPU)
            depth_map = self.model.infer(image_bgr)

            # Store result
            self.results[request_id] = depth_map

    def start_async_depth(self, image_bgr, request_id):
        # Deep copy to prevent shared memory issues
        self.queue.put((request_id, image_bgr.copy()))

    def get_depth(self, request_id):
        return self.results.get(request_id)
#+END_SRC

This reduces perceived depth processing time from 150-300ms (blocking) to 12-20ms (async).

*** Request Throttling and Adaptive Frame Rate

**** Dynamic Frame Interval Adjustment
The system adjusts frame capture rate based on server performance:
#+BEGIN_SRC javascript
const timingConfig = {
    CPU_MODE_INTERVAL: 800,     // 1.25 FPS for CPU
    GPU_MODE_INTERVAL: 150,     // 6.7 FPS for GPU
    DETECTION_TIMEOUT: 5000     // 5 second timeout
};

// Detect mode from first response
function adjustFrameRate(processingTime) {
    if (processingTime > 200) {
        currentInterval = timingConfig.CPU_MODE_INTERVAL;
        console.log('Switching to CPU mode frame rate');
    } else {
        currentInterval = timingConfig.GPU_MODE_INTERVAL;
        console.log('Switching to GPU mode frame rate');
    }
}
#+END_SRC

*** Selective Object Tracking

Only objects that need collision analysis are tracked with DeepSORT:
- Always track: person, car, bicycle, motorcycle, truck, bus, dog, cat
- Track if close: bbox area > 3% of frame (likely within collision distance)
- Never track: tiny objects, far objects, static background

This reduces tracking overhead by 40-60% while maintaining safety.

*** Memory Management and Cleanup

**** GPU Context Cleanup on Shutdown (run.py:24-48)
Proper cleanup prevents "CUDA initialization failure" on restart:
#+BEGIN_SRC python
def cleanup_and_exit(signum=None, frame=None):
    print("Cleaning up GPU resources...")

    # Stop depth estimator async worker
    if _depth_estimator is not None:
        _depth_estimator.cleanup()

    # Clear CUDA cache
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        torch.cuda.empty_cache()

    sys.exit(0)

# Register signal handlers
signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, cleanup_and_exit)   # Ctrl+C
signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, cleanup_and_exit)  # kill command
#+END_SRC

**** Automatic Tracker Cleanup
DeepSORT trackers are automatically cleaned up every 100 requests to prevent memory leaks from abandoned sessions.

** Feature Summary

*** Core Detection Features
- Real-time object detection: 600+ classes from Open Images V7 dataset
- Confidence thresholding: Filters low-quality detections (< 30%)
- Class normalization: Maps similar classes (man/woman → person)
- Spatial deduplication: Removes overlapping same-class detections
- Blacklist filtering: Removes non-useful classes (buildings, towers, etc.)

*** Depth and Distance Features
- Monocular depth estimation: Depth Anything V2 Small (14ms GPU)
- Three-tier distance estimation: Deep learning → Known heights → Bbox heuristic
- Motion-based calibration: Converts relative depth to metric distance
- Distance confidence scoring: Indicates reliability of estimates
- 500+ object heights database: Realistic measurements for common objects

*** Tracking and Movement Features
- Persistent object tracking: DeepSORT with MobileNetV2 embeddings
- Trajectory visualization: Last 30 positions for each tracked object
- Movement analysis: Speed classification (slow/medium/fast/stationary)
- Direction detection: Perspective-aware (approaching/moving away/lateral)
- Selective tracking optimization: Only track collision-relevant objects

*** Collision Detection Features
- Trajectory extrapolation: Predicts object paths 10 frames ahead
- User zone definition: 15% radius around frame center
- Time-to-collision calculation: Warns N seconds before impact
- Priority-based warnings: Critical audio for imminent collisions
- Moving object emphasis: Extra penalties for dynamic obstacles

*** Navigation Features
- Reactive local navigation: Real-time obstacle avoidance (500ms updates)
- 16-direction sampling: Finds safest path toward goal
- Vision-based position tracking: Visual odometry from depth-tracked objects
- Voice guidance generation: Natural language navigation instructions
- Tap-to-navigate interface: Set goals by tapping on video

*** Audio Features
- Text-to-speech output: Web Speech API with configurable voice
- Three-level priority queue: Critical/High/Normal announcements
- Global rate limiting: Minimum 800ms between announcements
- Per-class cooldowns: Prevents spam for frequently detected objects
- Spatial audio: Directional announcements (left/right/front)
- Movement announcements: User-centric perspective (approaching/moving away)

### Voice Control Features
- Continuous speech recognition: Always listening for commands
- Navigation commands: "find bench", "find door", "find chair"
- Control commands: "start", "stop", "audio on/off"
- Query commands: "what do you see", "describe scene"
- Fuzzy object matching: Partial name matches (e.g., "door" matches "wooden door")
- Closest object selection: Automatically navigates to nearest match

*** Interface Features
- Real-time visualization: Canvas-based bounding box rendering
- Color-coded classes: Red (person), Yellow (vehicle), Blue (furniture), Green (other)
- Trajectory trails: Visual history of object movements
- Collision indicators: Red pulsing borders with warning icons
- Navigation overlay: Goal markers and safe direction arrows
- Orientation indicators: Gyroscope status display

*** Camera Features
- Gyroscope-based rotation correction: Automatic orientation handling
- HTTPS support: Self-signed certificates for camera access
- Adaptive frame rate: Adjusts to server performance (CPU vs GPU)
- Request ID tracking: Prevents stale response rendering
- Timeout handling: Detects and recovers from network issues

** Architectural Advantages

*** Why Client-Server Architecture

The distributed architecture was chosen for specific technical reasons:

**** GPU Processing Power
- Mobile GPUs lack the VRAM and compute for YOLOv8x (requires 4GB+ VRAM)
- Server GPU (RTX 5060/4060/2050) provides 10-16 FPS vs 0.5-1 FPS on mobile
- Thermal throttling on mobile devices causes 50% performance drop after 2-3 minutes

**** Model Accuracy
- YOLOv8x (136MB) achieves 53% mAP on COCO
- YOLOv8n (6MB mobile-friendly) only achieves 37% mAP
- Quantization (FP32 → INT8) drops accuracy by 25%
- For blind navigation, accuracy is more critical than standalone operation

**** Easy Updates
- Model updates deployed to server only (no app store approval)
- Configuration changes take effect immediately
- Algorithm improvements benefit all users instantly

**** Platform Independence
- Works on any device with a modern browser
- No separate iOS/Android app development needed
- Consistent experience across all platforms

*** Why Reactive Navigation Over A-Star

Traditional A-star pathfinding was replaced with reactive navigation because:

**** A-Star Assumptions
- Assumes static environment (obstacles don't move)
- Requires complete knowledge of environment
- Calculates path once, then follows it
- Replanning is expensive and causes jerkiness

**** Dynamic Environment Reality
- People walk around continuously
- Furniture gets moved
- Doors open and close
- New obstacles appear constantly

**** Reactive Navigation Advantages
- Updates every 500ms with fresh obstacle data
- No replanning needed - always calculates fresh direction
- Handles moving obstacles naturally
- Smooth guidance without path invalidation issues

The A-star implementation is kept for future use in semi-static environments (e.g., empty office at night).

** Footnotes

[fn:16] HTTPS is required for camera access in modern browsers. The system uses self-signed SSL certificates for local deployment (cert.pem and key.pem).

[fn:17] The detection endpoint is the most complex part of the backend, orchestrating YOLO detection, depth estimation, object tracking, and collision prediction in a single request.

[fn:18] TensorRT provides the most dramatic performance improvement in the entire system - a 10-15x speedup compared to CPU inference, making real-time blind navigation possible.


* Dataset Used
** Online dataset 
[[Open Images V7][https://storage.googleapis.com/openimages/web/index.html]] by Google is a dataset of ~9M images annotated with image-level labels, object bounding boxes, object segmentation masks, visual relationships, and localized narratives:
Contains around 600 object categories and 2.8M bounding box annotations.

** Root Directory Files
*** run.py
#+begin_src python
"""
Application entry point for the blind navigation Flask application.

Usage:
    python run.py

Environment Variables:
    FLASK_ENV: 'development', 'production', or 'testing' (default: 'development')
    FLASK_HOST: Host address (default: from config)
    FLASK_PORT: Port number (default: from config)
    SSL_CERT: Path to SSL certificate (default: 'cert.pem')
    SSL_KEY: Path to SSL key (default: 'key.pem')
"""

import os
import signal
import sys
from app import create_app, log_startup_info

# Create the Flask application
app = create_app()


def cleanup_and_exit(signum=None, frame=None):
    """Gracefully cleanup resources before exit."""
    print("\n\nShutting down server...")
    print("Cleaning up GPU resources...")

    try:
        # Import here to avoid circular imports
        from app.services.depth_estimator import _depth_estimator

        # Cleanup depth estimator if it exists
        if _depth_estimator is not None:
            print("Stopping async depth worker...")
            _depth_estimator.cleanup()
            print("Depth estimator cleaned up successfully")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during cleanup: {e}")

    print("Shutdown complete")
    sys.exit(0)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    # Register signal handlers for graceful shutdown
    signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, cleanup_and_exit)  # Ctrl+C
    signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, cleanup_and_exit)  # kill command

    # Log startup information
    log_startup_info(app)

    # Get configuration from app config
    host = app.config.get('HOST', '192.168.1.14')
    # host = app.config.get('HOST', '10.242.173.242')
    port = app.config.get('PORT', 5000)
    debug = app.config.get('DEBUG', False)
    use_ssl = app.config.get('USE_SSL', True)

    # SSL context
    ssl_context = None
    if use_ssl:
        ssl_cert = app.config.get('SSL_CERT', 'cert.pem')
        ssl_key = app.config.get('SSL_KEY', 'key.pem')

        # Check if SSL files exist
        if os.path.exists(ssl_cert) and os.path.exists(ssl_key):
            ssl_context = (ssl_cert, ssl_key)
            print(f"🔒 SSL enabled with cert: {ssl_cert}")
        else:
            print(f"⚠️  SSL files not found ({ssl_cert}, {ssl_key}), running without SSL")

    # Run the application
    print(f"\n🌐 Starting server on {host}:{port}")
    print(f"🔧 Debug mode: {'ON' if debug else 'OFF'}")
    print(f"🔒 SSL: {'ENABLED' if ssl_context else 'DISABLED'}")
    print("\n" + "=" * 70 + "\n")

    app.run(
        debug=debug,
        threaded=True,
        host=host,
        port=port,
        use_reloader=False,  # Disable reloader to avoid model loading twice
        ssl_context=ssl_context
    )
#+end_src

#+begin_src python
"""
Flask application configuration.
"""

import os
from dotenv import load_dotenv

# Load environment variables from .env file
load_dotenv()


class Config:
    """Base configuration."""

    # Flask settings
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get('SECRET_KEY') or 'VerySecretKey'

    # CORS settings
    CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get('CORS_ORIGINS', '*')
    CORS_METHODS = ['GET', 'POST', 'OPTIONS']
    CORS_ALLOW_HEADERS = ['Content-Type']

    # Model settings
    # NOTE: .env file overrides these defaults. Set MODEL_PATH=yolov8n-oiv7.engine for TensorRT
    MODEL_PATH = os.environ.get('MODEL_PATH', 'yolov8n-oiv7.engine')  # Default to TensorRT for best performance
    FORCE_CPU = os.environ.get('FORCE_CPU', 'false').lower() == 'true'  # Use GPU by default if available (RTX 5060)

    # Server settings
    # HOST = os.environ.get('FLASK_HOST', '192.168.1.14')
    HOST = os.environ.get('FLASK_HOST', '10.242.173.242')
    PORT = int(os.environ.get('FLASK_PORT', 5000))
    DEBUG = os.environ.get('FLASK_DEBUG', 'false').lower() == 'true'

    # SSL settings
    SSL_CERT = os.environ.get('SSL_CERT', 'cert.pem')
    SSL_KEY = os.environ.get('SSL_KEY', 'key.pem')
    USE_SSL = os.environ.get('USE_SSL', 'true').lower() == 'true'

    # Accessibility settings for blind users
    BLIND_MODE_ENABLED = os.environ.get('BLIND_MODE_ENABLED', 'true').lower() == 'true'
    AUTO_START_DETECTION = os.environ.get('AUTO_START_DETECTION', 'true').lower() == 'true'
    VOICE_COMMANDS_ENABLED = os.environ.get('VOICE_COMMANDS_ENABLED', 'true').lower() == 'true'
    AUDIO_FEEDBACK_ENABLED = os.environ.get('AUDIO_FEEDBACK_ENABLED', 'true').lower() == 'true'

    # Audio settings for visually impaired users
    SPEECH_RATE = float(os.environ.get('SPEECH_RATE', '1.0'))  # Speech rate (0.5 to 2.0)
    SPEECH_PITCH = float(os.environ.get('SPEECH_PITCH', '1.0'))  # Speech pitch (0.0 to 2.0)
    SPEECH_VOLUME = float(os.environ.get('SPEECH_VOLUME', '1.0'))  # Volume (0.0 to 1.0)

    # Detection announcement settings
    ANNOUNCE_DELAY_MS = int(os.environ.get('ANNOUNCE_DELAY_MS', '3000'))  # Delay between announcements
    ANNOUNCE_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD = float(os.environ.get('ANNOUNCE_PRIORITY_THRESHOLD', '0.7'))  # Min confidence
    ANNOUNCE_COLLISION_WARNINGS = os.environ.get('ANNOUNCE_COLLISION_WARNINGS', 'true').lower() == 'true'


class DevelopmentConfig(Config):
    """Development configuration."""
    DEBUG = True
    # Inherit FORCE_CPU from parent Config (respects .env file)


class ProductionConfig(Config):
    """Production configuration."""
    DEBUG = False
    # In production, you might want to enable GPU if available
    FORCE_CPU = False


class TestingConfig(Config):
    """Testing configuration."""
    TESTING = True
    FORCE_CPU = False


# Configuration dictionary
config = {
    'development': DevelopmentConfig,
    'production': ProductionConfig,
    'testing': TestingConfig,
    'default': DevelopmentConfig
}


def get_config(env=None):
    """
    Get configuration based on environment.

    Args:
        env: Environment name ('development', 'production', 'testing')
             If None, uses FLASK_ENV environment variable or 'default'

    Returns:
        Config: Configuration class
    """
    if env is None:
        env = os.environ.get('FLASK_ENV', 'default')
    return config.get(env, config['default'])

#+begin_src python
"""
Camera management routes for overseer dashboard.
Handles camera registration, status updates, and communication.
"""

from flask import Blueprint, jsonify, request
from app.services.camera_registry import get_camera_registry
from app.services.server_monitor import get_monitor
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

bp = Blueprint('cameras', __name__, url_prefix='/admin/cameras')


@bp.route('/register', methods=['POST'])
def register_camera():
    """Register a new camera client."""
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        camera_id = data.get('camera_id')

        # Get client info
        client_info = {
            'ip': request.remote_addr,
            'user_agent': request.headers.get('User-Agent', 'Unknown'),
            'name': data.get('camera_name', f'Camera {camera_id[:8] if camera_id else "New"}')
        }

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.register_camera(camera_id=camera_id, client_info=client_info)

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'camera_id': camera.camera_id,
            'camera': camera.to_dict(),
            'message': 'Camera registered successfully'
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error registering camera: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/unregister/<camera_id>', methods=['POST'])
def unregister_camera(camera_id: str):
    """Unregister a camera client."""
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()
        success = registry.unregister_camera(camera_id)

        if success:
            return jsonify({
                'success': True,
                'message': f'Camera {camera_id} unregistered'
            })
        else:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error unregistering camera: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/heartbeat/<camera_id>', methods=['POST'])
def camera_heartbeat(camera_id: str):
    """Update camera heartbeat and state."""
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        registry = get_camera_registry()

        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)
        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found. Please register first.'
            }), 404

        # Update camera state
        registry.update_camera_state(camera_id, data)

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Heartbeat received',
            'camera': camera.to_dict()
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error processing heartbeat: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/list', methods=['GET'])
def list_cameras():
    """Get list of all registered cameras."""
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()

        # Cleanup stale cameras before listing
        registry.cleanup_stale_cameras()

        stats = registry.get_statistics()

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'statistics': {
                'total_cameras': stats['total_cameras'],
                'online_cameras': stats['online_cameras'],
                'offline_cameras': stats['offline_cameras'],
                'total_detections': stats['total_detections'],
                'total_frames': stats['total_frames']
            },
            'cameras': stats['cameras']
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error listing cameras: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>', methods=['GET'])
def get_camera(camera_id: str):
    """Get detailed information about a specific camera."""
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'camera': camera.to_dict()
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error getting camera: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/command', methods=['POST'])
def send_camera_command(camera_id: str):
    """
    Send a command to a specific camera.
    Commands: start_detection, stop_detection, toggle_audio, toggle_pathfinding
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        command = data.get('command')
        params = data.get('params', {})

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        # Store command for camera to poll
        # In a real implementation, this would use WebSocket to push to camera
        # For now, we just log it
        logger.info(f"Command '{command}' sent to camera {camera_id} with params: {params}")

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': f'Command {command} sent to camera {camera_id}',
            'command': command,
            'params': params
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error sending command: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/message', methods=['POST'])
def send_message_to_camera(camera_id: str):
    """
    Send a text/audio message to a specific camera.
    This will be announced via TTS on the camera.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        message = data.get('message')

        if not message:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Message text is required'
            }), 400

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        # In real implementation, push message via WebSocket
        logger.info(f"Message sent to camera {camera_id}: {message}")

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Message sent successfully',
            'camera_id': camera_id,
            'text': message
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error sending message: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/broadcast', methods=['POST'])
def broadcast_message():
    """
    Broadcast a message to all online cameras.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        message = data.get('message')

        if not message:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Message text is required'
            }), 400

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        online_cameras = registry.get_online_cameras()

        # In real implementation, push to all cameras via WebSocket
        logger.info(f"Broadcasting message to {len(online_cameras)} cameras: {message}")

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Message broadcast successfully',
            'recipients': len(online_cameras),
            'text': message
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error broadcasting message: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/stream', methods=['GET'])
def get_camera_stream(camera_id: str):
    """
    Get the latest frame from a camera for dashboard viewing.
    Returns base64 encoded JPEG frame.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        # Check if streaming is enabled
        if not camera.dashboard_streaming_enabled:
            return jsonify({
                'success': True,
                'streaming_enabled': False,
                'message': 'Camera streaming is disabled by user'
            })

        # Get latest frame
        frame_data = camera.get_latest_frame()

        if frame_data:
            return jsonify({
                'success': True,
                'streaming_enabled': True,
                'frame': frame_data,
                'fps': camera.current_fps,
                'timestamp': camera.last_frame_time
            })
        else:
            return jsonify({
                'success': True,
                'streaming_enabled': True,
                'frame': None,
                'message': 'No frame available yet'
            })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error getting camera stream: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/upload_frame', methods=['POST'])
def upload_camera_frame(camera_id: str):
    """
    Upload a frame from the camera client for dashboard streaming.
    Camera clients should send frames periodically when streaming is enabled.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        frame_data = data.get('frame')

        if not frame_data:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Frame data is required'
            }), 400

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found. Please register first.'
            }), 404

        # Store the frame
        camera.update_frame(frame_data)

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Frame uploaded successfully'
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error uploading frame: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/voice/start', methods=['POST'])
def start_voice_session(camera_id: str):
    """
    Start a voice communication session with a specific camera.
    This clears any existing audio queue and prepares for new voice chunks.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        camera.start_voice_session()

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Voice session started',
            'camera_id': camera_id,
            'camera_name': camera.camera_name
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error starting voice session: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/voice/chunk', methods=['POST'])
def upload_voice_chunk(camera_id: str):
    """
    Upload a voice audio chunk to be played on the camera.
    Chunks should be sent in sequence with incrementing sequence numbers.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        data = request.get_json() or {}
        audio_data = data.get('audio')
        sequence = data.get('sequence', 0)

        if not audio_data:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Audio data is required'
            }), 400

        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        # Queue the audio chunk
        camera.queue_audio_chunk(audio_data, sequence)

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Audio chunk uploaded',
            'sequence': sequence,
            'queue_length': len(camera.audio_chunks_queue)
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error uploading voice chunk: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/<camera_id>/voice/stop', methods=['POST'])
def stop_voice_session(camera_id: str):
    """
    Stop a voice communication session with a specific camera.
    """
    monitor = get_monitor()
    monitor.record_request()

    try:
        registry = get_camera_registry()
        camera = registry.get_camera(camera_id)

        if not camera:
            return jsonify({
                'success': False,
                'error': 'Camera not found'
            }), 404

        camera.stop_voice_session()

        return jsonify({
            'success': True,
            'message': 'Voice session stopped',
            'camera_id': camera_id
        })

    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"Error stopping voice session: {str(e)}")
        monitor.record_error()
        return jsonify({
            'success': False,
            'error': str(e)
        }), 500


@bp.route('/broadcast/voice/start', methods=['POST'])
def start_voice_broadcast():
    """
    Start a voice broadcast to all online cameras.

@bp.route('/broadcast/voice/chunk', methods=['POST'])
